# DEEPSEEK REVIEW — sweep_reclaim vs prereg (adversarial)

Scope: code read only. Runtime numbers (n=407, +0.020, CI, placebo 95th, thr=0.031, p90/p95 cuts, NQ pt scale) are REPORTED to me, NOT verifiable by reading code — flagged as [RUNTIME].

## (1) Lookahead — essentially clean; two minor notes
VERIFIED:
- Level point-in-time: ONL = mm(n0,a) = [prev 18:00, 09:30) (script:17,19); PDL/PDH = prev RTH mm(pa,pb) (script:19); IBL/IBH = mm(a,ib1)=[09:30,10:30) (script:17,19). Start times stored in lv[nm][2] = 575/575/630 (script:20); detection begins at max(s.a+5, s.a+startMin-570) (script:27) → ONL/PDL at 09:35, IBL at 10:30. All levels fully known before their detection start. No leak.
- First penetration `c(i-1)>=lv && lo(i)<lv` (script:27) uses only prior close + current low → no gap-through starts, no lookahead.
- Reclaim within M: `for j=i0; j<=i0+Mm ... if c(j)>=lv {td=j;break}` (script:29); decision time = that close. NO_RECLAIM sets td=i0+Mm (script:30); the reclaim loop already scanned j up to i0+M inclusive, so c(td)<lv at decision → "still beyond the level" holds. Only info ≤ t_d used.
- Depth threshold thr = percentile(DQ=50) of explore-only first-penetration depths (script:37, `e.d < SPLIT`), frozen, then applied to hold-out via `sel` (script:38). Explore-only — correct.
MINOR: (a) thr pools BOTH signs and BOTH classes (RECLAIM+NO_RECLAIM). Prereg says "all first penetrations" → faithful, but a low-sweep cutoff arguably should use the low-sweep distribution. (b) drift tables built over ALL non-roll sessions incl. hold-out and event sessions (script:23) — but prereg line 22 says "all non-roll sessions", so full-sample drift is pre-registered, not an accidental leak.

## (2) Mirror (high-sweep) sign handling — VERIFIED correct, no bug
- lv=sgn*level, c=sgn*C, lo = L (sgn>0) / -H (sgn<0) (script:26). Mirror penetration: c(i-1)>=lv ⇔ C[i-1]<=high; lo(i)<lv ⇔ H[i]>high (script:27). Mirror reclaim: c(j)>=lv ⇔ C[j]<=high (script:29). depth=(lv-mn)/atr with mn=min(-H)=-max(H) ⇒ positive (script:31).
- Return/drift: Rk = sgn*(C[td+k]-C[td])/atr; Ak = Rk - sgn*drift[bucket(td)] (script:32). For sgn=-1: A = -(C change)/atr + drift = short_return - (-drift) = short_return minus short-direction drift. Fair; matches prereg "subtract dir*(C[t+k]-C[t])/ATR20". No sign error.

## (3) Drift adjustment — fair but coarse; no fatal bias
VERIFIED: drift[k][bucket] = mean of (C[i+k]-C[i])/atr over minutes i with i+k<s.b (RTH-clipped, script:23); bucket=(mod-570)/30, 30-min bins (script:24). Event side requires td+k<s.b (script:32) → same RTH clipping, so late-day exclusion symmetric. Long subtracts long drift; short subtracts short drift. Correct direction.
CAVEATS: (a) 30-min bucket by START time: 09:35 vs 09:55 events share one drift though the k-window crosses buckets — coarse, not biased. (b) full-sample drift (see (1)) — negligible over-fit (events ~407 vs ~millions of minutes). (c) [INFER] near-close events drop out for k=120/240 (td+k>=s.b), so long-horizon coverage skews morning — coverage asymmetry, not a drift bias.

## (4) Shifted-level placebo — biased null (the real problem, next to (6))
VERIFIED mechanics: allEvents(shift=true) adds ±(0.25+0.75*rnd())·atr per level per sign (script:35), reruns detect(), and line 48 filters with the SAME frozen thr via sel → placebo uses the real, trivial-poke threshold (0.031 ATR). Prereg-mandated "same depth threshold" — faithful.
[INFER] bias in the NULL: real ONL/PDL/IBL are EXTREMA (price actually touched them); a shifted level generally is NOT an extremum. Shift "toward" price (up for lows) puts the level inside the traded range → trivial crossings; shift "away" (down for lows) puts it below the overnight low where price often never reaches → no event (deep move if it does); shifted levels price opens "beyond" are dropped by the gap-through rule (c(i-1)>=lv). Net: placebo event mix = interior level-crossings, NOT extreme-level sweeps. It tests "arbitrary interior crossing" (≈0 edge) rather than the claimed "sweep of a defended extreme". Generous in isolation, but with the 0.031 threshold both arms collapse to ~0 → powerless.

## (5) Day-blocked bootstrap + criterion (iv) — faithful
VERIFIED: dci (script:40) groups by day, resamples days WITH replacement (ds.length draws), mean = sum/count, 2000 draws, 2.5/97.5 → proper cluster bootstrap, matches prereg. Criterion (iv) (script:49): sums A120 per session, sorts desc, removes top 5 session-sums, remainder>0 — matches "positive after removing 5 best sessions". All five criteria c1..c5 (script:50) map 1:1 to prereg (i)-(v). MINOR: removes by session TOTAL (not per-event mean), so a high-event-count day dominates its own sum; unspecified but reasonable.

## (6) NOT-SUPPORTED is a construction artifact: the threshold IS the flaw
VERIFIED: thr = median of explore first-penetration depths (script:37). By definition the median is the "typical poke" depth, so "qualifying" = deeper than half of all pokes — admits ~half the poke distribution and pools genuine deep sweeps with ~10pt pokes. The [RUNTIME] thr≈0.031 ATR20 vs the user's 0.4-ATR example is ~13× shallower; that number follows directly from the median-of-first-penetrations definition even though I can't recompute it. So n≈407 [RUNTIME] is dominated by trivial pokes and the real-sweep case is diluted toward zero edge. The [RUNTIME] p90/p95 negative cuts (n=24/10) are exploratory, tiny, and can't rescue or refute anything.

Corrected design for the DEEP-sweep case, without data-mining:
1. Fixed a-priori cutoff from the origin story: depth ≥ 0.25 (or 0.4) ATR20. No data-derived threshold — cleanest, but n will be small.
2. Adaptive, point-in-time (better): per-session "real sweep" = depth ≥ pre-registered P-th percentile of the trailing N-session distribution of overnight-low violation depth (for each prior session: max depth price traded below ONL, else 0). Pre-register N (e.g. 60) and P (e.g. 90) BEFORE outcomes; compute only from strictly-prior data; verify on explore, test on hold-out. Volatility-scaled, anchored to "unusually deep violation", freezable.
3. Apply the SAME corrected threshold to the placebo (deep sweep of a shifted level) and report n per arm.

VERDICT: the code faithfully implements the prereg; no lookahead and no mirror-sign bug found. The fatal issue is in the prereg itself — "median depth of first penetrations" is a poke-scale threshold that dilutes the deep-sweep case, so NOT SUPPORTED does not license any conclusion about the real 0.4-ATR sweep-and-reclaim edge.

The review is complete and written to `scratch/deepseek_response.md` (lines 552–588). Summary of my findings:

## What I verified by reading code (with file:line)

**(1) Lookahead — clean.** Levels are point-in-time: ONL `[prev 18:00, 09:30)` (script:17,19), PDL = prev RTH (script:19), IBL `[09:30,10:30)` (script:19). Detection starts 09:35 for ONL/PDL, 10:30 for IBL (script:20,27). First-penetration uses only prior close + current low (script:27); reclaim uses info ≤ t_d (script:29-30). The depth threshold is explore-only (`e.d < SPLIT`, script:37) then frozen and applied to hold-out (script:38) — correct. Two minor notes: the threshold pools both signs/classes, and drift is full-sample (but pre-registered as "all non-roll sessions").

**(2) Mirror sign — correct, no bug.** `lv=sgn*level`, `lo = -H` for shorts, reclaim `c(j)>=lv ⇔ C[j]<=high`, `depth` positive, and `A = Rk - sgn*drift` gives `short_return - (-drift)` (script:26,29,31,32).

**(3) Drift — fair but coarse.** RTH-clipped on both sides (script:23,32), correct per-direction subtraction. Caveats: 30-min bucket coarseness and morning coverage skew for long horizons.

**(4) Placebo — biased null.** Mechanics faithful (script:35,48), but shifted levels are interior crossings, not extreme-level sweeps — a different null than the claim.

**(5) Bootstrap + criterion (iv) — faithful.** Proper day-blocked cluster bootstrap (script:40); all five criteria map 1:1 to pre-reg (script:50).

**(6) The verdict is a construction artifact.** `thr` = **median** of explore first-penetration depths (script:37) — by definition the "typical poke" scale (~0.031 ATR vs the user's 0.4 ATR example). This dilutes the deep-sweep case into trivial pokes. I proposed a corrected design: a fixed a-priori cutoff (0.25/0.4 ATR), or better, a pre-registered trailing-N-session percentile of overnight-low violation depth, computed point-in-time.

**Key verdict:** the code faithfully implements the pre-registration (no lookahead, no mirror bug), but the pre-reg's "median of first penetrations" threshold is the fatal flaw — so NOT SUPPORTED does not license any conclusion about the real deep-sweep-reclaim edge. Runtime outputs (n=407, +0.020, CI, thr=0.031, p90/p95) were labeled as reported/unverifiable, as instructed.
